### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

An oxide of iron contains $69.9\%$ iron, its empirical formula, is: (Given: Molar mass of Fe and O are $56$ and $16$ g mol$^{-1}$ respectively.)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text{FeO}$
- **(B)** $\text{Fe}_2\text{O}_3$
- **(C)** $\text{Fe}_3\text{O}_4$
- **(D)** $\text{FeO}_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\text{Fe}_2\text{O}_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass percentage of $\text{Fe} = 69.9\%$

Mass percentage of $\text{O} = 100\% - 69.9\% = 30.1\%$

Number of moles of $\text{Fe} = \dfrac{69.9}{56} = 1.248$

Number of moles of $\text{O} = \dfrac{30.1}{16} = 1.881$

Molar ratio of $\text{Fe} : \text{O} = 1.248 : 1.881$

Dividing by the smallest value, the ratio is $\dfrac{1.248}{1.248} : \dfrac{1.881}{1.248} = 1 : 1.5$

Multiplying by $2$ to get the simplest whole number ratio, we get $2 : 3$.

The empirical formula is $\text{Fe}_2\text{O}_3$.

Answer: $\text{Fe}_2\text{O}_3$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

What volume of hydrogen gas at STP would be liberated by action of $50\text{ mL}$ of $H_2SO_4$ of $50\%$ purity (density $= 1.3\text{ g mL}^{-1}$) on $20\text{ g}$ of zinc ?
Given : Molar mass of H, O, S, Zn are $1, 16, 32, 65\text{ g mol}^{-1}$ respectively.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.824\text{ L}$
- **(B)** $7.428\text{ L}$
- **(C)** $6.892\text{ L}$
- **(D)** $8.375\text{ L}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $6.892\text{ L}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of $H_2SO_4$ solution $= 50 \times 1.3 = 65\text{ g}$

Mass of pure $H_2SO_4 = 65 \times \dfrac{50}{100} = 32.5\text{ g}$

Moles of $H_2SO_4 = \dfrac{32.5}{98} \approx 0.3316\text{ mol}$

Moles of $Zn = \dfrac{20}{65} \approx 0.3077\text{ mol}$

The balanced chemical equation is:
$Zn + H_2SO_4 \rightarrow ZnSO_4 + H_2$

Since the moles of $Zn$ are less than the moles of $H_2SO_4$, $Zn$ is the limiting reagent.

Moles of $H_2$ produced $=$ Moles of $Zn = \dfrac{20}{65}\text{ mol}$

Volume of $H_2$ gas at STP $= \dfrac{20}{65} \times 22.4\text{ L} = 6.892\text{ L}$

Answer: $6.892\text{ L}$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the given reaction: 
 $\mathrm{CaCO}_{3}+2 \mathrm{HCl} \longrightarrow \mathrm{CaCl}_{2}+\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}+\mathrm{CO}_{2}$ 
 If $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{CaCO}_{3}$ is added to 300 mL of HCl which contains $38.55 \% \mathrm{HCl}$ by mass and has density $1.13 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mL}^{-1}$, then which of the following option is correct ? 
 Given molar mass of $\mathrm{H}, \mathrm{Cl}, \mathrm{Ca}$ and O are 1, 35.5, 40 and $16 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ respectively.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 64.97 g of HCl remains unreacted
- **(B)** 60.32 g of HCl remains unreacted
- **(C)** $97.30 \mathrm{~g} \text { of } \mathrm{HCl} \text { reacted }$
- **(D)** 32.85 g of $\mathrm{CaCO}_{3}$ remains unreacted

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 64.97 g of HCl remains unreacted**

**Detailed Solution:**

Molar masses: HCl = 36.5, CaCO₃ = 100 g/mol
HCl solution: mass = 300 × 1.13 = 339 g
HCl present: 339 × 0.3855 = 130.74 g = 130.74/36.5 = 3.58 mol
CaCO₃: 90/100 = 0.9 mol
Reaction: $CaCO_3 + 2HCl → CaCl_2 + H_2O + CO_2$
HCl required: 0.9 × 2 = 1.8 mol
HCl available: 3.58 mol > 1.8 mol
CaCO₃ is limiting. HCl remaining: (3.58 - 1.8) × 36.5 = 1.78 × 36.5 = 64.97 g

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$\mathrm{A}+2 \mathrm{~B} \longrightarrow \mathrm{AB}_{2}$ 
 36.0 g of 'A' (Molar mass: $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$) and 56.0 g of ' $\mathrm{B}^{\prime}$ (Molar mass: $80 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$) are allowed to react. Which of the following statements are correct ? 
 A. 'A' is the limiting reagent. 
 B. $77.0 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{AB}_{2}$ is formed. 
 C. Molar mass of $A B_{2}$ is $140 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. 
 D. $15.0 \mathrm{~g}$ of A is left unreacted after the completion of reaction. 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** C and D Only
- **(B)** A and B Only
- **(C)** A and C Only
- **(D)** B and D Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) B and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reaction: A + 2B → AB₂.
Moles of A = 36.0/60 = 0.6 mol;
Moles of B = 56.0/80 = 0.7 mol.
A requires 1.2 mol of B but only 0.7 available, so B is limiting reagent. 
So, (A) False - B is limiting.
(B) AB₂ formed = 0.7/2 = 0.35 mol;
Molar mass AB₂ = 60 + 2(80) = 220 g/mol;
Mass = 0.35 × 220 = 77 g True.
(C) False - Molar mass is 220 g/mol.
(D) A remaining = 0.6 - 0.35 = 0.25 mol = 15 g True.
Correct statements: B and D.

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In the reaction, 
 $2 \mathrm{Al}(\mathrm{s})+6 \mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{aq}) \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Al}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq})+6 \mathrm{Cl}^{-}(\mathrm{aq})+3 \mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $11.2 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ at STP is produced for every mole of HCl consumed.
- **(B)** $33.6 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ is produced regardless of temperature and pressure for every mole of Al that reacts.
- **(C)** $12 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{aq})$ is consumed for every $6 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ produced.
- **(D)** $67.2 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ at STP is produced for every mole of Al that reacts.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $11.2 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ at STP is produced for every mole of HCl consumed.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reaction: $2\text{Al}(s) + 6\text{HCl}(aq) \rightarrow 2\text{Al}^{3+}(aq) + 6\text{Cl}^-(aq) + 3\text{H}_2(g)$
Checking each option:
(1) Per 1 mole HCl: From stoichiometry, 6 mol HCl produces 3 mol H₂, so 1 mol HCl produces 0.5 mol H₂ = 11.2 L at STP. TRUE
(2) Per 1 mole Al: 2 mol Al produces 3 mol H₂, so 1 mol Al produces 1.5 mol H₂ = 33.6 L at STP only, not regardless of T and P. FALSE
(3) Volume ratio at same T,P: 6 mol HCl : 3 mol H₂ = 2:1, so 12 L HCl : 6 L H₂. TRUE
(4) Per 1 mole Al: 1.5 mol H₂ = 33.6 L, not 67.2 L. FALSE
Option (1) is correct.

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Aqueous HCl reacts with $\mathrm{MnO}_{2}(\mathrm{~s})$ to form $\mathrm{MnCl}_{2}(\mathrm{aq}), \mathrm{Cl}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ and $\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}(l)$. What is the weight (in g) of $\mathrm{Cl}_{2}$ liberated when 8.7 g of $\mathrm{MnO}_{2}(\mathrm{~s})$ is reacted with excess aqueous HCl solution? 
 (Given Molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{Mn}=55, \mathrm{Cl}=35.5, \mathrm{O}=16, \mathrm{H}=1$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 21.3
- **(B)** 71
- **(C)** 14.2
- **(D)** 7.1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 7.1**

**Detailed Solution:**

The balanced redox equation is: $\text{MnO}_2 + 4\text{HCl} \rightarrow \text{MnCl}_2 + \text{Cl}_2 + 2\text{H}_2\text{O}$.
Molar mass of MnO₂ = 55 + 32 = 87 g/mol.
Moles of MnO₂ = $\frac{8.7}{87} = 0.1$ mol.
From stoichiometry, 1 mol MnO₂ produces 1 mol Cl₂, so 0.1 mol produces 0.1 mol Cl₂.
Mass of Cl₂ = $0.1 \times 71 = 7.1$ g.

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

80 mL of a hydrocarbon on mixing with 264 mL of oxygen in a closed U-tube undergoes complete combustion. The residual gases after cooling to 273 K occupy 224 mL. When the system is treated with KOH solution, the volume decreases to 64 mL. The formula of the hydrocarbon is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{2}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{4}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{6}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{C}_{4} \mathrm{H}_{10}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

KOH absorbs $CO_2$. Volume of $CO_2$ produced = $224 - 64 = 160$ mL
Unreacted $O_2 = 64$ mL, so $O_2$ consumed = $264 - 64 = 200$ mL
For hydrocarbon $C_xH_y$: $\frac{CO_2}{\text{Hydrocarbon}} = \frac{160}{80} = 2 \Rightarrow x = 2$
$O_2$ consumed per mole = $x + \frac{y}{4} = \frac{200}{80} = 2.5$
$2 + \frac{y}{4} = 2.5 \Rightarrow y = 2$
Hydrocarbon is $C_2H_2$

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

14.0 g of calcium metal is allowed to react with excess HCl at 1.0 atm pressure and 273 K. Which of the following statements is incorrect? 
 [Given : Molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ of $\mathrm{Ca}-40, \mathrm{Cl}-35.5, \mathrm{H}-1$]

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.35 mol of $\mathrm{H}_{2}$ gas is evolved.
- **(B)** 7.84 L of $\mathrm{H}_{2}$ gas is evolved.
- **(C)** The limiting reagent is calcium metal.
- **(D)** 33.3 g of $\mathrm{CaCl}_{2}$ is produced.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 33.3 g of $\mathrm{CaCl}_{2}$ is produced.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reaction: $Ca + 2HCl \rightarrow CaCl_2 + H_2$
Moles of Ca = $\frac{14.0}{40} = 0.35$ mol. Since HCl is in excess, Ca is limiting.
Option (1): $H_2$ evolved = 0.35 mol 
Option (2): Volume of $H_2$ at STP = $0.35 \times 22.4 = 7.84$ L 
Option (3): Ca is limiting reagent 
Option (4): Mass of $CaCl_2 = 0.35 \times 111 = 38.85$ g, not 33.3 g 
Statement (4) is incorrect.

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2025 (8 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

20 mL of sodium iodide solution gave 4.74 g silver iodide when treated with excess of silver nitrate solution. The molarity of the sodium iodide solution is ________ M. (Nearest Integer value) (Given : $\mathrm{Na}=23, \mathrm{I}=127, \mathrm{Ag}=108, \mathrm{~N}=14$, $\mathrm{O}=16 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{NaI}_{(\mathrm{aq})}+\mathrm{AgNO}_{3(\mathrm{aq)}} \rightarrow \mathrm{AgI}_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{NaNO}_3(\mathrm{aq}) \\
& \mathrm{M}, 20 \mathrm{ml} \text { excess } \\
& 4.74 \mathrm{~g}
\end{aligned}$
$\text { Moles of } \mathrm{I}^{-} \text {in } \mathrm{NaI}=\text { Moles of }\left(\mathrm{I}^{-}\right) \text {in } \mathrm{AgI}=\frac{4.74}{235}$
$\text { Moles of } \mathrm{NaI}=\frac{4.74}{235}$
$\text { Molarity }[\mathrm{NaI}]=\frac{4.74}{235 \times 0.02}=1.008$

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Butane reacts with oxygen to produce carbon dioxide and water following the equation given below
$\mathrm{C}_4 \mathrm{H}_{10}(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{13}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow 4 \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+5 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l})$
If 174.0 kg of butane is mixed with 320.0 kg of $\mathrm{O}_2$, the volume of water formed in litres is ________.(Nearest integer)
[Given : (a) Molar mass of $\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{H}, \mathrm{O}$ are 12, 1, $16 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ respectively, (b) Density of water $\left.=1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mL}^{-1}\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **138**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{C}_4 \mathrm{H}_{10}+\frac{13}{2} \mathrm{O}_2 \rightarrow 4 \mathrm{CO}_2+5 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \\
& 3 \times 10^3 \quad 10 \times 10^3
\end{aligned}$
Moles of $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$ formed $=\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}}=5 \times \frac{2}{13} \times 10 \times 10^3$
Then $\mathrm{w}_{\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}}=\frac{10^5}{13} \times 18$
$=1.3846 \times 10^5 \mathrm{~g}$
Volume of $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$ will be $=138.46$ litre.
Ans. 138

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An organic compound weighing 500 mg , produced 220 mg of $\mathrm{CO}_2$. on complete combustion. The percentage composition of carbon in the compound is _____ \%. (nearest integer)
(Given molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ of $\mathrm{C}: 12, \mathrm{O}: 16$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **12**

**Detailed Solution:**

Organic compound $\xrightarrow[\Delta]{\mathrm{CuO}} \mathrm{CO}_2+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{CO}_2}=\frac{220 \times 10^{-3}}{44}=5 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{moles} \\ & \mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{C}}=5 \times 10^{-3} \times 12\end{aligned}$
$\% \mathrm{~m}$ carbon $=\frac{5 \times 10^{-3} \times 12}{500 \times 10^{-3}} \times 100=12 \%$
Correct answer is $12$

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The amount of calcium oxide produced on heating 150 kg limestone ( $75 \%$ pure) is ______ kg. (Nearest integer)
Given : Molar mass (in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$) of Ca-40, O-16, C-12

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **63**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{CaCO}_3 \rightarrow \mathrm{CaO}+\mathrm{CO}_2 \\ & \text { mass of } \mathrm{CaCO}_3=\frac{150 \times 75}{100}=112.5 \mathrm{~kg} \\ & =112500 \mathrm{~g} \\ & \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{CaCO}_3}=1125 \\ & \mathrm{So} \text { moles of } \mathrm{CaO}=1125 \\ & \quad \text { mass of } \mathrm{CaO}=\frac{1125 \times 56}{1000}=63 \mathrm{~kg} \\ & \text { Correct answer } \Rightarrow 63\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Mass of magnesium required to produce 220 mL of hydrogen gas at STP on reaction with excess of dil. HCl is
Given : Molar mass of Mg is $24 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 235.7 g
- **(B)** 0.24 mg
- **(C)** 236 mg
- **(D)** 2.444 g

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 236 mg**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Mg}+2 \mathrm{HCl} \rightarrow \mathrm{MgCl}_2+\mathrm{H}_2$
Volume $\mathrm{H}_2$ evolved $=220 \mathrm{ml}$
Mole of $\mathrm{H}_2=\frac{220 \times 10^{-3}}{22.4}=$ mole of Mg used
$\begin{aligned}
\therefore \text { Mass of Mg used } & =\frac{220 \times 10^{-3}}{22.4} \times 24 \\
& =235.7 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{gm} \\
& =235.7 \mathrm{mg}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

On complete combustion 1.0 g of an organic compound (X) gave 1.46 g of \(\mathrm{CO}_2\) and 0.567 g of \(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\). The empirical formula mass of compound \((\mathrm{X})\) is ________ g.
(Given molar mass in \(\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{C}: 12, \mathrm{H}: 1, \mathrm{O}: 16\))

**Options:**

- **(A)** 30
- **(B)** 45
- **(C)** 60
- **(D)** 15

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 30**

**Detailed Solution:**

Moles of ' C ' \(=\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{CO}_2}=\frac{1.46}{44}=0.033\)
Moles of ' C ' \(=\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{C}}=0.033 \times 12\)
Moles of 'H' \(=2 \times \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}}=2 \times \frac{0.567}{18}=0.063\)
Mass of ' H ' \(=0.0063\)
Mass of Oxygen \((\mathrm{O})=1-\left(\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{c}}+\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{H}}\right)\) \(=1-(0.033 \times 12+0.063 \times 1)=0.541 \mathrm{gm}\)
Moles of ' O ' \(=\frac{0.541}{16}=0.033\)
Empirical formula \(=\mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{O}\)
Empirical formula mass \(=30\).

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

\(\mathrm{CaCO}_3(\mathrm{~s})+2 \mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{aq}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CaCl}_2(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l})\)
Consider the above reaction, what mass of \(\mathrm{CaCl}_2\) will be formed if 250 mL of 0.76 M HCl reacts with 1000 g of \(\mathrm{CaCO}_3\) ?
(Given : Molar mass of \(\mathrm{Ca}, \mathrm{C}, \mathrm{O}, \mathrm{H}\) and Cl are 40, \(12,16,1\) and \(35.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\), respectively)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3.908 g
- **(B)** 2.636 g
- **(C)** 10.545 g
- **(D)** 5.272 g

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 10.545 g**

**Detailed Solution:**

\(\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{CaCO}_3+2 \mathrm{HCl} \rightarrow \mathrm{CaCl}_2+\mathrm{CO}_2+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \\
& \text { Moles of } \mathrm{CaCO}_3=\frac{1000}{100}=10
\end{aligned}\)
Moles of \(\mathrm{HCl}=0.76 \times \frac{250}{1000}=0.19\) (L.R.)
Moles of \(\mathrm{CaCl}_2\) formed \(=\frac{0.19}{2}\)
Mass of \(\mathrm{CaCl}_2=\frac{0.19}{2} \times 111=10.545 \mathrm{gm}\)

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

Choose the correct statements.
(A) Weight of a substance is the amount of matter present in it.
(B) Mass is the force exerted by gravity on an object.
(C) Volume is the amount of space occupied by a substance.
(D) Temperatures below \(0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\) are possible in Celsius scale, but in Kelvin scale negative temperature is not possible.
(E) Precision refers to the closeness of various measurements for the same quantity.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

- **(A)** (A), (D) and (E) Only
- **(B)** (C), (D) and (E) Only
- **(C)** (A), (B) and (C) Only
- **(D)** (B), (C) and (D) Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (C), (D) and (E) Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of substance is amount of matter present in it. Weight is force exerted by gravity on object.

### Q19. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Quantitative analysis of an organic compound (X) shows following % composition.

C : $14.5 \%$
Cl : 64.46%
H: 1.8 %
(Empirical formula mass of the compound $(\mathrm{X})$ is __________ $\times 10^{-1}$
(Given molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ of $\mathrm{C}: 12, \mathrm{H}: 1, \mathrm{O}: 16, \mathrm{Cl}: 35.5$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1655**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll:cccl} & \mathrm{C} & \mathrm{Cl} & \mathrm{H} & \mathrm{O} &  \\ \text { %mass } & 14.5 & 64.46 & 1.8 & 19.24\end{array}$
Molar ratio $\frac{14.5}{12} \quad \frac{64.46}{35.5} \quad \frac{1.8}{1} \quad \frac{19.24}{16}$


$\begin{aligned}
&\begin{array}{ccccc} 
& 1.2 & 1.8 & 1.8 & 1.2 \\
\text { Minimum } & 2 & 3 & 3 & 2
\end{array}\\
&\text { integral ratio }
\end{aligned}$

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Empiricial formula }=\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_3 \mathrm{Cl}_3 \mathrm{O}_2 \\ & \text { Mass }=165.5 \\ & \text { Mass }=1655 \times 10^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The elemental composition of a compound is $54.2 \% \mathrm{C}, 9.2 \% \mathrm{H}$ and $36.6 \% \mathrm{O}$.
If the molar mass of the compound is $132 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, the molecular formula of the compound is :
[Given : The relative atomic mass of $\mathrm{C}: \mathrm{H}: \mathrm{O}=12: 1: 16$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{C}_4 \mathrm{H}_9 \mathrm{O}_3$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_{12} \mathrm{O}_6$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{C}_4 \mathrm{H}_8 \mathrm{O}_2$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_{12} \mathrm{O}_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_{12} \mathrm{O}_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/-2MbQJb__JWl80JiUWFf4SJQJeUgWBOye1NDQndmhRI.original.fullsize.png">

Molecular mass of compound $=132 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ Molecular formula of compound is $\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_4 \mathrm{O}\right)_n$
$\mathrm{n}=\frac{\text { Molecular mass }}{\mathrm{EF} \text { mass }}=\frac{132}{44}=3$
$\therefore$ Molecular formula of compound is $\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_{12} \mathrm{O}_3$

</details>

---

### Q21. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following reaction occurring in the blast furnace:
$\mathrm{Fe}_3 \mathrm{O}_{4(\mathrm{~s})}+4 \mathrm{CO}_{(\mathrm{g})} \rightarrow 3 \mathrm{Fe}_{(\mathrm{l})}+4 \mathrm{CO}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
' $x$ ' kg of iron is produced when $2.32 \times 10^3 \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{Fe}_3 \mathrm{O}_4$ and $2.8 \times 10^2 \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{CO}$ are brought together in the furnace. The value of ' $x$ ' is _____. (nearest integer)
Given:
molar mass of $\mathrm{Fe}_3 \mathrm{O}_4=232 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
molar mass of $\mathrm{CO}=28 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
molar mass of $\left.\mathrm{Fe}=56 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right\}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **420**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \text { moles of } \mathrm{Fe}_3 \mathrm{O}_4=\frac{2.32 \times 10^3 \times 10^3}{232}=10000 \mathrm{~mol} \\
& \text { moles of } \mathrm{CO}=\frac{2.8 \times 10^2 \times 10^3}{28}=10000 \mathrm{~mol} \\
& \mathrm{Fe}_3^3 \mathrm{O}_4+4 \mathrm{CO} \longrightarrow 3 \mathrm{Fe}+4 \mathrm{CO}_2 \\
& 10^4 \mathrm{~mol} \quad 10^4 \mathrm{~mol}
\end{aligned}$

CO is L.R.
mole of $\mathrm{Fe}=\frac{3}{4} \times 10^4$
mass of $\mathrm{Fe}=\frac{3}{4} \times \frac{10^4 \times 56}{1000} \mathrm{~kg}=420 \mathrm{~kg}$

</details>

---

### Q22. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Combustion of glucose \(\left(\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_{12} \mathrm{O}_6\right)\) produces \(\mathrm{CO}_2\) and water. The amount of oxygen (in g) required for the complete combustion of \(900 \mathrm{~g}\) of glucose is :
[Molar mass of glucose in \(\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}=180\) ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** 480
- **(B)** 800
- **(C)** 960
- **(D)** 32

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 960**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_{12} \mathrm{O}_{6(\mathrm{~s})}+6 \mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \longrightarrow 6 \mathrm{CO}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}+6 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}_{(\theta)} \\ & \frac{900}{180} \\ & =5 \mathrm{~mol} \quad 30 \mathrm{~mol}\end{aligned}$
Mass of $\mathrm{O}_2$ required $=30 \times 32=960 \mathrm{gm}$

</details>

---

### Q23. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The number of moles of methane required to produce \(11 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})\) after complete combustion is :
(Given molar mass of methane in \(\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}: 16\))

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.35
- **(B)** 0.5
- **(C)** 0.75
- **(D)** 0.25

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 0.25**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{n}} \mathrm{H}_{2 \mathrm{n}+2}+\frac{3 \mathrm{n}+1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2 \longrightarrow \mathrm{nCO}_2+(\mathrm{n}+1) \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/AYEL2ItwKFHNNN0K_HIiXtQu3Rk1OuRI1F2YvlKPPVc.original.fullsize.png">
$0.25 \mathrm{~mol~} \mathrm{CH}_4$ gives $0.25 \mathrm{~mole}$ (or $11 \mathrm{gm}$ ) $\mathrm{CO}_2$

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

\(9.3 \mathrm{~g}\) of pure aniline is treated with bromine water at room temperature to give a white precipitate of the product ' \(\mathrm{P}\) '. The mass of product ' \(\mathrm{P}\) ' obtaind is \(26.4 \mathrm{~g}\). The percentage yield is _______ \(\%\).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **80**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7jiMnvP4kq8rOxiX3eEXIJ8LXj-kryXMS87h6wF8kU8.original.fullsize.png">
$93 \mathrm{~g}$ of aniline produces $330 \mathrm{~g}$ of $2,4,6-$ tribromoaniline. Hence $9.3 \mathrm{~g}$ of aniline should produce $33 \mathrm{~g}$ of 2, 4, 6-tribromoaniline. Hence percentage yield $\frac{26.4 \times 100}{33}=80 \%$

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

An organic compound has \(42.1 \%\) carbon, \(6.4 \%\) hydrogen and remainder is oxygen. If its molecular weight is 342, then its molecular formula is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{C}_{11} \mathrm{H}_{18} \mathrm{O}_{12}\)
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{C}_{12} \mathrm{H}_{20} \mathrm{O}_{12}\)
- **(C)** \(\mathrm{C}_{12} \mathrm{H}_{22} \mathrm{O}_{11}\)
- **(D)** \(\mathrm{C}_{14} \mathrm{H}_{20} \mathrm{O}_{10}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

only $\mathrm{C}_{12} \mathrm{H}_{22} \mathrm{O}_{11}$ has $42.1 \%$ carbon, $6.4 \%$ hydrogen $\& 51.5$ percent oxygen.

### Q26. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of gaseous hydrocarbon on combustion gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of water vapour. Total number of carbon and hydrogen atoms in the hydrocarbon is _______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **14**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The combustion reaction can be represented as </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>xCO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>yH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p>At STP, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole of any gas occupies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>22</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of hydrocarbon, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of water and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of carbon dioxide corresponds to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>23</mn></math>,  and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>79</mn><mo> </mo></math>moles respectively.</p><p>Hence, the combustion of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole of hydrocarbon will produce <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> moles of water and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> moles of carbon dioxide.</p><p>Thus, the molecular formula contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> carbon atoms and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn></math> hydrogen atoms.</p><p>The hydrocarbon is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>10</mn></msub></math> and the combustion reaction is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>10</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>→</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math></p><p>Hence the Total number of carbon and hydrogen atoms in the hydrocarbon is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Consider the following reaction:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>Pb</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math></p><p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>72</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mmol</mi><mo> </mo><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is mixed with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mmol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math> then amount of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Pb</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math> formed in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mmol</mi></math>. (nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **24**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The reaction is given as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>Pb</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math></p><p>The products formed in the above reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Pb</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo> </mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math>  .</p><p>According to the above stoichiometric equation, three mole of each lead chloride and two moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is required to make one mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Pb</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext> and</mtext><mo> </mo><mn>6</mn><mtext> moles of</mtext><mo> </mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math>   </p><p>Here the Limiting Reagent is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mmol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Pb</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></math> formed</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>mmol</mi><mtext> of </mtext><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext> reacted </mtext></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>72</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn><mo> </mo><mi>m mol</mi></math></p>

The composition of the mixture is:</p><p>(Given molar mass in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CaCO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>MgCO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mn>84</mn></math>)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.187</mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>C</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.023</mo><mo> </mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.023</mo><mo> </mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>C</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.023</mo><mo> </mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.187</mo><mo> </mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>C</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.187</mo><mo> </mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.023</mo><mo> </mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>C</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.187</mo><mo> </mo><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

### Q30. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PbS</mi></math> is oxidised by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> to get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>.</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given that, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PbS</mi></math> is oxidised by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> to get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>The balanced chemical equation can be written as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PbS</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>PbSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo></math></p><p>Hence,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The volume of hydrogen liberated at STP by treating <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of magnesium with excess of hydrochloric acid is ......<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math>. Given Molar volume of gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>22</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> at STP. Molar mass of magnesium is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **224**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The stoichiometric equation of the reaction between magnesium and hydrogen chloride can be written as follows,</p><p>One mole magnesium can liberate one mole of hydrogen gas according to the following equation.</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><munder><mi>Mg</mi><munder><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mole</mi></mrow></munder></munder><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>HCl</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><munder><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mole</mi></mrow></munder></math>
Volume of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></math> mole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> at STP will occupy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of silver nitrate solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is added dropwise to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of potassium iodide <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>)</mo></math> solution. The ion(s) present in very small quantity in the solution is/are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> only
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>only
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>only
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>both

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>both**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>To determine the ion(s) present in very small quantity in the solution, we need to consider the chemical reaction that occurs when silver nitrate reacts with potassium iodide. The reaction between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KI</mi></math> is a double replacement reaction and can be represented as follows:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KI</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>AgI</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>KNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p>Millimoles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p>Millimoles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KI</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>KI</mi></math> is in excess &amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgI</mi></math> forms negatively charged colloid. (Some <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>remains in solution) lons <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>&amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>are therefore, present in very small quantity.</p>

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is mixed with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math> the maximum number of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Ba</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math>formed is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi></math> (Nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The balanced chemical equation for the reaction between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Ba</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>NaCl</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> moles of barium chloride will require <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></math> moles of sodium phosphate.</p><p>Here, sodium phosphate is the limiting reagent.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> moles of sodium phosphate produce <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole of barium phosphate.</p><p>Thus,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> moles of sodium phosphate will produce<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> mole of barium phosphate.</p><p>Hence, the maximum no. of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Ba</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub></math>that can be formed is one mole.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q36. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Assume carbon burns according to following equation :
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>CO</mi><mtext>&#160;(s)&#160;</mtext></math>
when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> carbon is burnt in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of oxygen, the volume of carbon monoxide produced is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> at STP [nearest integer]</p><p>[Given : Assume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> as ideal gas, Mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and molar volume of an idal gas at STP is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>22</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ]</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **227**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Combustion of carbon under less supply of air,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>CO</mi></math>    ....(X)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>12</mn></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>48</mn><mn>32</mn></mfrac></math>                (Given moles of reactants)
Thus, carbon is the limiting reagent as for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of Oxygen, 3 moles of carbon is required.</p><p>Thus, from equation(X) we get,</p><p>Mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> mole</p><p>From arrhenius law,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi>CO</mi></msub></mfenced><mi>atSTP</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>Lit</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>227</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi>Lit</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q37. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

When a hydrocarbon <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> undergoes complete combustion it requires <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>11</mn></math> equivalents of oxygen and produces <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> equivalents of water. What is the molecular formula of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>11</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>11</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>9</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>9</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>By using the formula of combustion analysis:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math>,</p><p>then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>8</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>11</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>8</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math></p><p>Formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>9</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Number of hydrogen atoms per molecule of a hydrocarbon A having <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>85</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>%</mo></math> carbon is (Given : Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>84</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **12**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using table of empirical formula for the compound:<table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td>Element</td>			<td>Percentage</td>			<td>Mole</td>			<td>ratio</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>85</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>85</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>H</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>14</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p> </p><p>Thus, empirical formula is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced></math>, empirical molecular mass = 14
Thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>Molecular</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Mass</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>Empirical</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Mass</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>84</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Molecular formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>12</mn></msub></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></msub><mo>&#8652;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p><p>Consider the above reaction. If $20$g of dinitrogen reacts with $5$g of dihydrogen, then the limiting reagent of the reaction and number of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> formed respectively are</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math> moles
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>71</mn></math> moles
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math> moles
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>71</mn></math> moles

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math> moles**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></msub><mo>&#8652;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Given</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mass</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Number</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></math></p><p>To find the limiting reagent we can divide given number of moles with Stoichiometric coefficients, whichever will be the lower will be limiting reagent.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is the Limiting Reagent.</p><p>According to the reaction one mole of nitrogen gas gives two moles of ammonia.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Number of moles of ammonia formed will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>In the given reaction,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>&#8644;</mo><msub><mi>XYZ</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p>if one mole of each of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> gives compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>XYZ</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>. (Given : Atomic masses of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> are 10,20 and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mi>amu</mi></math>, respectively). The yield of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>XYZ</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Z is present in least amount so, it will be limiting reagent and amount of product will depend on the amount of Z.  </p><p>          <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi ### Q41. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Production of iron in blast furnace follows the following equation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>CO</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>Fe</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>640</mn><mi>kg</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>520</mn><mi>kg</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> are allowed to react then the amount of iror (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>) produced is :</p><p>[Given: Molar Atomic mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mfenced><mo>:</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>56</mn></math></p><p>Molar Atomic mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>gmolm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mfenced><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math></p><p>Molar Atomic mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>gmolm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mfenced><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></math>]</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1400</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2200</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3360</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4200</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3360</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>640</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mn>232</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn></math></p><p>Moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>90</mn></math></p><p>So limiting Reagent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math></p><p>So moles of Fe formed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn></math></p><p>Weight of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3360</mn><mi>gms</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q44. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>116</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a substance upon dissociation reaction, yields <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of hydrogen, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of oxygen and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>48</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of carbon. Given that the atomic masses of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>16</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn></math>, respectively. The data agrees with how many formulae of the following?</p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></math></p><p>B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCHO</mi></math></p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>OOCH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p>D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>CHO</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>Organic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Compound</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>containg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mrow><mi>dissociati</mi><mo>on</mo></mrow></mover><munder><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>gram</mi></mrow></munder><mo>+</mo><munder><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>60</mn><mi>gram</mi></mrow></munder><mo>+</mo><munder><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mn>48</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>gram</mi></mrow></munder></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> in the given organic compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>116</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>465</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p><p>(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>60</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p><p>(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>30</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></math>%</p><p>(c) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mn>62</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>677</mn></math>%</p><p>(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>44</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>09</mn></math>%</p><p>The percentage of Hydrogen in the given organic compound is equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>465</mn><mo>%</mo></math> which is approximately equal to the percentage of hydrogen in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HCHO</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CNG</mi></math> is an important transportation fuel. When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>gCNG</mi></math> is mixed with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>208</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> oxygen in vehicles, it leads to the formation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> and produces large quantity of heat during this combustion, then the amount of carbon dioxide, produced in grams is [nearest integer] [Assume CNG to be methane]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **143**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#10230;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mn>16</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>208</mn><mn>32</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>Number</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>stoichiometric</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>coefficient</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>for</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mfrac><mrow><mi>Number</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>stoichiometric</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>coefficient</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>for</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p>The reagent with lower ratio of number of moles to stoichiometric coefficient is called limiting reagent.</p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is limiting reagent</p><p>Limiting reagent decides the amount of product,</p><p>As per the equation 2 moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> gives rise to 1 mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>, So 6.5 moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>nCO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>143</mn><mi>gm</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> aqueous solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of this solution is diluted with deionized water to give <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>. In solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> are added. Total <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> mols of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> in the final solution is____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **20**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Initial moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> (in/Lit.) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn></math></p><p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>%</mo></math> solution moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></math></p><p>Added moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></math></p><p>Total moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> in resulting solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> m moles</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> milimoles</p>

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> atoms in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>681</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>7</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>21</mn></msup></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>23</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> (Nearest Integer)

### Q52. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 1)

(in nearest integer)

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

</details>
