### Q82. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>A gaseous hydrocarbon on combustion gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. What is the empirical formula of the hydrocarbon?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>7</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>7</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>y</mtext> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac><mrow><mtext>y</mtext> <mo></mo> </mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac> </mrow></mfenced> <msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>&#10230;</mo> <mtext>xC</mtext> <msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>y</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>7</mn> <mtext>&#160;moles</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>7</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>&#160;moles</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>y</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>07</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> atoms of carbon

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> atoms of hydrogen</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>07</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p><p>Empirical formula becomes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>7</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Experimentally it was found that a metal oxide has formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>0.98</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>. Metal M, is present as <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> in its oxide. Fraction of the metal which exists as <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> would be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mn>5</mn><mtext>%</mtext></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mn>8</mn><mtext>%</mtext></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mn>1</mn><mtext>%</mtext></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mn>8</mn><mtext>%</mtext></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mn>8</mn><mtext>%</mtext></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Consider one mole of the oxide, then

Moles of M = 0.98;     Moles of O<sup>2- </sup>= 1

Let &#39;x&#39; moles of M are in +3 oxidation state, then Moles of M<sup>2+</sup> = 0.98-x

On doing charge balancing we get 

(0.98 - x)2 + 3x  = 2

Or 1.96 - 2x + 3x - 2 = 0

Or x = 0.04

 <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756; %</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.04</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.98</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.08</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">%</mi></math>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A transition metal $M$ forms a volatile chloride which has a vapour density of $94.8$. If it contains $74.75 \%$ of chlorine the formula of the metal chloride will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{MCl}_3$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{MCl}_2$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{MCl}_4$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{MCl}_5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{MCl}_4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$74.75 \%$ of chlorine means $74.75 \mathrm{~g}$ chlorine is present in $100 \mathrm{~g}$ of metal chloride.
Weight of metal $=100 \mathrm{~g}-74.75 \mathrm{~g}$ $=25.25 \mathrm{~g}$
Equivalent weight
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{\text { weight of metal }}{\text { weight of chlorine }} \times 35.5 \\
& =\frac{25.25}{74.75} \times 35.5=12
\end{aligned}
$$
Valency of metal
$$
\begin{array}{r}
=\frac{2 \times \text { V.D. }}{\text { Equivalent wt. of metal }+35.5} \\
=\frac{2 \times 94.8}{12+35.5}=4
\end{array}
$$
$\therefore$ Formula of compound $=\mathrm{MCl}_4$

</details>

---

### Q85. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$5 \mathrm{~g}$ of benzene on nitration gave $6.6 \mathrm{~g}$ of nitrobenzene. The theoretical yield of the nitrobenzene will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.5 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(B)** $5.6 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(C)** $8.09 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(D)** $6.6 \mathrm{~g}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $8.09 \mathrm{~g}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6+\mathrm{HNO}_3 \rightarrow \mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_5 \mathrm{NO}_2+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$ $78 \mathrm{~g} \quad 123 \mathrm{~g}$ Now since $78 \mathrm{~g}$ of benzene on nitration give $=123 \mathrm{~g}$ nitrobenzene hence $5 \mathrm{~g}$ of benzene on nitration give $=\frac{123}{78} \times 5=7.88 \mathrm{~g}$
nearest answer is (c) i.e. theoritical yield $=7.88 \mathrm{~g}$

</details>
